115 年 8 月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
8 月實徵淨額 1,982 億元，較上年同月增加 445 億元(+28.9%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額 3 兆 749 億元，較上年同期增加 6,191 億元
(+25.2%)。 
（三）達成率 
累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.0%。 
圖 1 全國賦稅收入及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動 
（一）8 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以證券交易稅增加 262 億元、營業
稅、營利事業所得稅及綜合所得稅分別增加 68 億元、66 億元及 61
億元較多。 
（二）累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 2,855
億元 、營利事業所得稅增加 1,599 億元及營業稅增加 906 億元較多。 
圖 2 主要稅目別變化 
(115 年 1-8 月) 
1.營利事業所得稅 
(1)8 月實徵淨額 132 億元，較上年同月增加 66 億元(+1.0 倍)，主因
大額退稅案件造成基期偏低所致。 
(2)累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額 8,021 億元，較上年同期增加 1,599 億元
(+24.9%)，主因結算申報自繳稅額增加及延分期稅款陸續入帳。 
2.綜合所得稅 
8 月實徵淨額 890 億元，較上年同月增加 61 億元(+7.4%)，主因薪資
所得扣繳稅款增加；累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額 7,790 億元，較上年同期
增加 757 億元(+10.8%)，主因結算申報自繳及盈餘分配 、 薪資所得等
扣繳稅款俱增所致。 
3.營業稅 
(1)8月因資通與視聽產品、電子零組件等進口代徵稅額增加，營業稅
實徵淨額較上年同月增加68億元，由負125億元縮小為負56億元。 
(2)累計1至8月實徵淨額4,816億元 ， 較上年同期增加906億元(+23.2%)

### Page 3

3
，主因內需穩定，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件等進口代徵稅額
增加，惟適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。
4.貨物稅
8月實徵淨額80億元，較上年同月減少43億元(-34.9%)，主因進口車
稅額減少及 汽柴油減徵幅度擴大 所致；累計1至8月實徵淨額 774億
元，較上年同期減少196億元(-20.2%)，除汽柴油降稅因素外，亦與
新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)8 月及累計 1 至 8 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 948 億
元、1 兆 1,606 億元，分別較上年同期(5,533 億元、4,247 億元)增
97.9%、1.7 倍。
(2)8 月 證 交稅 實徵 淨 額 540 億元 ，較 上 年同 月增加 262 億元
(+94.5%)；累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額 4,527 億元，較上年同期增加
2,855 億元(+1.7 倍)。
6.關稅
8 月實徵淨額 137 億元， 較上年同月增加9 億元(+7.4%)，累計 1 至 8

月實徵淨額 1,067 億元，較上年同期增加 77 億元(+7.8%)，均因機械
及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
8 月實徵淨額 32 億元，較上年同月增加 14 億元(+79.6%)，累計 1 至
8 月實徵淨額 188 億元，較上年同期增加 37 億元(+24.1%)，主因大
額稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
8 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 10 億元(+22.7%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額 448 億元，較上年同期減少
26 億元(-5.5%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
（三）前 8 月主要徵起稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(273%)、遺產稅(159%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(128%)、 關稅(116%)、 營業稅(110%)、 契稅(110%)、 房屋稅(106%)、
綜所稅(102%)、營所稅(102%)及牌照稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(98%)、地價稅(88%)、土增稅

(85%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

三、各級政府賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 8 月中央政府賦稅收入 (不含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵淨 額
2 兆 846 億元(占分配預算數 121.8%)，較上年同期增加 2,694 億元
(+14.8%)，雖新版財政收支劃分法 開始施行，營業 稅劃歸中央政府 比
率大幅調降，減少 2,128 億元(-90.8%)，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加 2,855
億元、1,969 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 8 月地方政府賦稅收
入(含中央統籌分配稅款)實徵
淨額 9,117 億元(占分配預算
數 106.3%)，較上年同期增加
3,541 億元(+63.5%)， 主因納入
中央統籌分配 之營業稅 增加
3,113 億元(+2.1 倍)所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 8 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 174 億元，較
上年同期增加 3 億元(+1.7%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 61
億元，較上年同期減少 1 億元(-2.2%)；撥入長照基金 551 億元(其中 243

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、174 億元來自菸稅，133 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 33 億元(+6.3%)。

顧
市場焦點
美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩 ，但就業回升至 50.1、訂單積壓增加 ，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩 ，反映燃料 、原物料及供應鏈成本升高 ，景氣未明顯轉弱之際 ，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高 ，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能 ，也提高資本需求 。
整體而言 ，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高 ，使金融條件維持偏緊 。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與 AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩 ，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵 ，加上長期通膨風險升溫 ，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金 。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易 ，實體黃金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至 59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩 ，但就業指數回升至 50.1、重返擴張區間 ，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高 ，通膨降溫面臨干擾： 價格指數升至 74.0，創2022年7月以來新高 ，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6 資料來源：ISM Services PMI Report， 2026.10.6

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心 、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6

獲利成長；中長期建議維持日股正向看法，聚焦金融、AI、機器人、自動化及電力設備等具結構性成長題材的企業。

中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境 ：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展 ，但也逐漸出現供給過剩 ，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰 。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻 、應用普及 ，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持 ，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源 。目前中國已備案大型語言模型 (LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署 ，市場進入門檻降低 ，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望： 考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭 ，未來三年中國 AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態 ，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源 、低成本 」模式 ，即使模型價格下降 ，AI 使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長 ，進而推升資料中心 、雲端服務及 AI晶片需求 ，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶 、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
中國AI市場規模預測
中國已備案的LLM數量

人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟
和市場信心
• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台 ，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心 ：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼 ，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期 ，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場 ，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業 ，納入重點扶持範圍 ，透過增加再貸款額度 ，與提高支持比例 ，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆 ，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮 ，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告 ，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險 ，數據顯示這項警告已成事實 。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化 ，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加 ，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整 (短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下 ，但目前中美利差顯著 (美中30年期公債利差超過 350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。
美國公債持倉：中國 vs 日本
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，美國財政部
中國預計將減少美元儲備
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，中國央行、國際貨幣基金組織、彭博經濟研究。

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹： 日本央行正加快升息腳步 。隨著日債殖利率上揚 (30年期日債殖利率升至約 4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降 ，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐 。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026.10.7。

兆豐銀行所做任何投資意見與市場分析結果，係依據資料製作當時情況進行判斷

1. 本資料內容僅供內部同仁及客戶參考，非作為任何金融商品或服務之購買或銷售之推介，不得視為買賣有價證券或其他金融商品之要約或要約之引誘。
2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
Overall September ODM NB shipments came in 2% below MSe: Quarter-end pull-
ins drove notebook shipments up 32% m/m, although volumes were 2% below MSe,
with weakness mainly coming from Wistron and Compal, and to a lesser extent,
Pegatron, reflecting slower NB shipments from Dell, as Dell accounted for ~60% of

Compal's NB shipment, and ~55% of Wistron's in CY25, on our estimates. Thus total
3Q NB builds came in at 25.2M units (-17% q/q, -26% y/y). This sequential decline
is sub-seasonal vs. the 15Y average seasonality of +5% q/q in 3Q.
Pull-forward demand appears to have come to an end, channel partners and
distributors have stocked up on inventory, and OEMs are slowing their sales into the
channel. We believe this is why our conversations with the ODMs suggest a slightly

weaker 4Q volume outlook than previously expected, despite favorable mix shift.
Thus, we lower our 4Q NB build estimate by 3% to 23.1M (-8% q/q, -29% y/y).
This sequential decline is sub-seasonal (despite an already lowered base in 3Q) vs.
the 15Y average seasonality of flat q/q in 4Q. We forecast October NB builds at
6.8M (-30% m/m, -33% y/y). Our revised forecasts imply CY26 builds of ~107.8M
units (-16% y/y) for the top five Taiwan NB ODMs.

Key Takeaways
Top five ODMs' September 2026 NB shipments were 9.7M units (+32% m/m, -
20% y/y), 2% below our estimate.
Total 3Q26 NB builds came in at 25.2M (-17% q/q, -26% y/y).
We see October 2026 NB builds at 6.8M units (-30% m/m, -33% y/y).
We lower our 4Q26 NB build estimate by 3% to 23.1M units (-8% q/q, -29% y/y).
Our top five ODMs' CY26 NB build estimate is ~107.8M units (-16% y/y).

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Genius Electronic Optical
Private placement with 3M expands its optical
connectivity opportunity
Genius approved a private placement with 3M
On 15th Oct, Genius's board approved a private placement to 3M UK Limited for
5.54mn shares at NT$869.6 per share, an 18.3% discount to the 14th Oct closing price
NT$1,065, representing NT$4.8bn investment and 4.68% stake in Genius. Proceeds
from the transaction will fund technology development, working capital, and bank loan

repayment. The two companies also announced a strategic partnership to develop
optical communication for AI data center and HPC. We believe this announcement is a
positive validation for Genius working with a tier one supplier in optical connectivity to
provide its precision components for advanced optics.
3M EBO technology could open up a US$100mn TAM for Genius
3M has been developing its EBO (expanded beam optical) technology since 2019 with
commercialization in late 2024 and deployment for Microsoft Azure data centers in July

2026. EBO is an upgrade to MPO connectors at the ends of fiber optics cables.  The
EBO is a lensed, non-contact ferrule that uses a prism lens to expand and collimate a
light beam to send across fiber before refocusing into a receiving fiber, improving dust
tolerance to cut cleaning steps and cable inspection times 85%. The technology was
supported by the MSA launched in May 2026, co-chaired by Oracle, with Meta, Senko
and Molex among the members for scale-out cabling. We saw opportunity opened for

Genius on the EBO ferrule, a small molded plastic part with a row of micro-lenses to
widen the light beam plus an anti reflection coating. This could open up a US$100mn
market TAM for Genius based on 4-12 ferrules per GPU (2-3 connections x 2-4 plugs per
connection) x 20mn accelerators x US$1 for the lens assembly.
Validation for its optical solution push with potential hyperscaler exposure
We believe the scale of placement at NT$4.8bn suggests scope to expand into CPO

where it could improve the MT ferrule used as the detachable plug end of the FAU to
hold the fiber in position when it is connected to a switch adapter or removable cable.
Genius  collaboration with 3M can bring the company closer to the end demand with its
direct partnership with Microsoft and bring revenue contribution forward to 2027,
ahead of Genius CPO ramp in 2028E. For CPO, we still see potential to reach 10% of the
TAM supplying MT ferrule, prism and V-groove.

Reiterate Buy with PT at NT$1,250
Our recent update on Genius had raised the PT from NT$700 to NT$1,250 reflecting the
company's potential CPO market with its progress sampling three precision components
inside the fiber array unit (FAU) targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.
The collaboration strengthens our confidence on Genius capability within the optical
connectivity industry, and reiterate our Buy on the compony.

Forecast returns

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model.
Genius' growth is highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by
volatility in handset demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their

camera design. Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of
new technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over

the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the

to a wider population. The phone also supports Apple pencil, which could also attract 
pencil users. (2) The latest technology with new form factor: hinges with more than

on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

OSAT 展望樂觀，SOIC 機台具技術優勢 
 2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT:2026 年弘塑產能擴充一倍 ，1Q26 已
經完成擴建 ，3Q26 新產能已經滿載。 外包部分 ， 目前外包比例33%，
未來佔比會下降，目前外包月產能一個月 5-6 台，因為外包產能受
限，同業也在搶外包產能。2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT，今年晶
圓代工客戶營收高於 OSAT，2027 年 OSAT 營收超過晶圓代工客戶，
SOIC 機台去年小量，今年量增，2027 跟 2026 差不多略增，2028
才會明顯大增，主要還是跟隨晶圓代工客戶 SOIC 擴廠進度。 
 2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量會很明顯放大:PLP 及 CoPoS 設備機
台單價很高，初估有機會是 CoWoS 翻倍，PLP 進度比較快，PLP
規格弘塑 310*310、510*515、600*600 都有，再大到 700 以上就不
做了，晶圓代工客戶規格是 310*310 但目前進度比較慢，還在實驗
線階段。力成 510*515 進度最快，4Q26 會拉第一批機台，2027 隨
力成購置的友達廠進度持續拉機台，2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量
會很明顯放大。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 3000 元：基本面無變化，預估數字也沒做調
整，故維持買進評等 ，預估 2026 年 EPS 64.81 元，2027 年 EPS 93.97
元，目標價 3000 元。(2027 PER 30x)

弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商，主要利基產品為半導體後段濕製程設備，並延
伸相關化學品、檢測設備代理及軟體解決方案。1H26 產品結構可區分為機台檢

(CLC)、2018 年底合併通路商佳霖(ClC)、併購資訊系統廠太引(Tyne)，投資美

2Q26 毛 利率 39.37%， 低於預
期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
 2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
弘塑 2Q26 EPS 11.18 元，低於元富預估的 12.34 元，獲利達成率 91%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 17.27 億元，+8%QOQ，+6%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，
較 1Q26 回升 5.1 個百分點，然仍低於預估的 41%，主要是外包成本及急單增
加影響部分毛利率，預期 3Q26 毛利率持續向上改善，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元。

弘塑 3Q26 營收 22.67 億元，+31.3%QOQ，+51.8%YOY，營收達成率 98%，
略低於預期，預估 3Q26 毛利率 41%，預估 3Q26 EPS 16.47 元。

基本面無變化，預估數字也沒做調整，OSAT 拉貨樂觀，長線 SOIC 機台放量可
期，故維持買進評等，預估 2026 年 EPS 64.81 元，2027 年 EPS 93.97 元，目
標價 3000 元。(2027 PER 30x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

### Page 1

현대차 (005380)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.16  
4분기 반등 기대

3Q26 Preview 
현대차는 3Q26E 매출액 42.4조원(-9.4% YoY, -13.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.5조원(-
2.8% YoY, -13.5% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 각 부문
별 영업이익은 자동차 1.7조원(-2.0% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 4.8%), 금융 5,501억
원(-4.5% YoY, -27.5% QoQ, OPM 6.9%)으로 예상한다. 
생산 정상화 및 신차 확대를 통한 수익성 개선 기대 
현대차는 글로벌 도매 판매 91.8만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 90.9
만대(-9.6%)를 기록했다. 7~8월에 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생산 차
질이 발생했다. 파업 영향으로 HEV 판매 증가율이 1%에 그치면서, HEV 판매 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선 효과도 시장 기대에 미치지 못했다.  
4분기부터는 3분기 파업 영향으로 지연된 생산량 회복 및 신차 확대가 본격화되면서, 
전분기 대비 매출 및 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 동사는 보급형 전기차 아이오닉3의 튀
르키예 생산을 9월부터 시작했으며, 이를 통해 유럽 시장 판매량은 점차 회복될 전망
이다. 또한, 1) HMGMA를 통한 HEV 미국 현지 생산 확대, 2) 제네시스 HEV 모델 순
차적 출시를 통해 내년부터 북미를 중심으로 믹스 개선이 본격화될 전망이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 520,000원으로 하향 
2027년 영업이익은 11.2조원(+10.2%)를 기록할 것으로 예상된다. 원화 약세 지속을 
반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다. 다만, 현재 주가는 
12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 수준이다. 1) RMAC 
성과 구체화, 2) RA(로봇 생산법인) 착공 등 Physical AI 사업이 구체화될 경우 밸류에
이션 할증이 가능할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

Document 203: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름 - 기업.pdf

 상대 (14.8) 6.0  (53.4)  
 절대 (달러환산) (7.9) 11.8  (4.8)

3Q26Pre: 환율 하락에도 성장 흐름 유지 
매출액 1조 3,477억원(+7.2%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 5,957억원(-6.0%, OPM 
44.2%)로 컨센서스에 부합할 것으로 전망. 3분기 원달러 평균 환율은 1,423원으로 전분기 
평균 환율 대비 약 5.2% 하락에도 불구하고 성장 흐름 지속. 
2분기 노조 파업으로 인한 영향은 3분기 생산을 진행했으며 4분기 매출에 반영될 예정으로 
연간 실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상. GSK로부터 인수한 록빌 공장도 안정적
으로 생산이 이루어지고 있으며 CAPA 여유분은 추가 수주를 진행 중으로 28~29년 이후 
완전 가동 전망. 
4Q, 6공장 건설과 폴리펩타이드 인수 
유상 증자 관련 투자 설명서 상 투자 계획에 따르면 6공장 투자는 26년부터 29년까지 진행 
될 예정으로 29년 가동 목표. 6공장은 5공장과 동일한 18만 리터 규모로 건설되며, 연내 투
자 집행을 시작함에 따라 건설 발표도 임박한 것으로 추정. 동사는 일정 수준 이상 기존 공
장 수주를 확보한 후 후속 공장 건설을 진행해오고 있어 non-binding 수주 계약의 본계약 
전환도 순차적으로 이루어질 것으로 전망. 
폴리펩타이드 인수도 현재 공개 매수를 진행 중에 있으며 연내 마무리될 것으로 예상. 
1H26 폴리펩타이드 매출은 2.37억 유로(+41.6%)로 GLP-1 agonist 등 펩타이드 의약품 
수요 증가에 따라 높은 성장 지속. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원으로 하향 
매수 의견을 유지하며 목표 주가는 기존 210만원에서 200만원으로 하향. 목표 주가 하향은 
유상 증자에 따른 EPS 희석에 기인. 글로벌 항체 CDMO 수요가 여전히 견조하며, 폴리펩
타이드 인수로 펩타이드로의 확장에 따른 추가적인 성장 기대. 주주 대상 유상 증자 영향과 
파업 우려 등이 있으나 노사 합의 시 주가 반등 전망.

Document 236: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-23).pdf

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, 20.45 điểm tương ứng 1.14%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Tiện ích khí là các nhóm ngành có mức tăng rõ nét nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị 2,680 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, HDB và BSR; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở 
mức 1,172 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại mua ròng 3,009 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 5,001 hợp đồng. Tự doanh bán ròng -9,259 hợp đồng và đang duy 
trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng 9,458 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần tăng điểm tích cực, chỉ số VNINDEX chinh phục thành công 
ngưỡng tâm lý 1.800 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện, độ rộng thị trường nghiêng về số mã 
tăng điểm và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài quay trở lại mua ròng. Động lực hỗ trợ thị trường 
trong tuần qua đến từ hoạt động cơ cấu của các quỹ bám theo rổ chỉ số mới nổi của FTSE, 
khi mà ngày 21/9 là ngày Việt Nam được chính thức nâng hạng. Thêm vào đó, diễn biến hạ 
nhiệt của giá dầu khi lo ngại thiếu hụt cung do xung đột ở Trung Đông giảm bớt cũng là 
yếu tố hỗ trợ tâm lý thị trường chung. Ở chiều ngược lại, thông tin FED tăng lãi suất có tác 
động tiêu cực nhưng ảnh hưởng không đáng kể lên thị trường do động thái này đã được 
dự báo từ trước.

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

Giá dầu duy trì ở mức cao, tiếp tục hỗ 
trợ biên lợi nhuận khí khô của GAS

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi mở rộng dư địa 
phát triển mỏ cận biên, tạo động lực 
tăng nguồn khí cho GAS trong dài 
hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
96,300 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng trưởng tích cực 44% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ (1) giá bán khí tăng 
và (2) tăng trưởng sản lượng tích cực từ mảng LNG. Trong khi đó, LNST-CĐTS tăng 
chậm hơn, ở mức 23% YoY, do ghi nhận khoản hoàn nhập dự phòng nợ phải thu khó 
đòi thấp hơn so với cùng kỳ năm trước.

Giá dầu Brent tăng trở lại lên vùng 95 USD/thùng trong bối cảnh xung đột Mỹ - Iran 
tiếp tục leo thang, làm gián đoạn nguồn cung dầu tại Trung Đông, trong khi lưu 
lượng tàu qua Eo biển Hormuz vẫn ở mức thấp và tồn kho dầu toàn cầu cùng Kho 
Dự trữ Chiến lược Mỹ liên tục suy giảm. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá dầu duy trì vùng 85 - 
95 USD/thùng trong ngắn hạn, qua đó tiếp tục hỗ trợ biên lợi nhuận mảng khí 
thượng nguồn của GAS nhờ chênh lệch giữa giá bán khí theo giá dầu và giá miệng 
giếng cố định tại các mỏ lâu đời.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi tăng ưu đãi cho các mỏ cận biên, giúp cải thiện hiệu quả đầu 
tư và thúc đẩy phát triển nguồn khí mới. GAS có thể hưởng lợi từ sản lượng khí bổ 
sung qua hệ thống hạ tầng hiện hữu, nhưng tác động chủ yếu trong trung và dài 
hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA để định giá cổ phiếu GAS, chúng tôi đưa 
ra khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 96,300 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức 
upside 16.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Giá khí đầu vào DCM 2028–2030F 
tăng do bổ sung nguồn khí giá cao từ 
Nam Du – U Minh

Luật Dầu khí mới cải thiện triển vọng 
nguồn khí đầu vào cho DCM trong 
dài hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
42,100 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng 10% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ giá bán urea tăng trong bối cảnh thị 
trường chịu tác động từ các yếu tố địa chính trị. LNST-CĐTS tăng 36% YoY, được hỗ 
trợ bởi giá bán đầu ra tăng nhanh hơn chi phí khí đầu vào.

Giá urea thế giới sau khi tăng cao trong Q2/2026 đã điều chỉnh về vùng trước chiến 
tranh do nhu cầu suy yếu và nguồn cung Trung Quốc quay trở lại. Đối với DCM, xuất 
khẩu urea tiếp tục là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính, trong khi giá và sản lượng 
urea nội địa được kỳ vọng đi ngang trong 2H2026 do đây không phải mùa cao điểm 
tiêu thụ trong nước.

Chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng 10% dự báo giá khí đầu vào DCM giai đoạn 2028–2030F, 
phản ánh việc bổ sung nguồn khí Nam Du – U Minh từ Q4/2028. Giá khí Nam Du – U 
Minh dự kiến 11.56 USD/mmBTU, cao hơn ~35% so với các nguồn hiện hữu, tạo áp 
lực tăng chi phí đầu vào.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi sẽ rút ngắn quy trình phê duyệt FDP và bổ sung ưu đãi cho các 
mỏ khó khai thác, qua đó hỗ trợ đầu tư E&P và duy trì sản lượng dầu khí trong 
nước. Đối với DCM, tác động dự kiến mang tính gián tiếp và có độ trễ, chủ yếu thông 
qua cải thiện nguồn cung khí nội địa, chưa phải động lực thúc đẩy lợi nhuận đáng 
kể trong ngắn hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu DCM chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 42,100 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
29.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Doanh thu và LNST-CĐTS 2Q2026 
tiếp tục tăng trưởng tốt, được hỗ trợ 
bởi đà tăng của crack spread

Biên lợi nhuận duy trì ở mức cao 
trong ngắn hạn nhờ crack spread 
bùng nổ, nhưng có thể biến động 
mạnh

Biên lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn chịu 
áp lực từ rủi ro dư cung dầu

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
32,200 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Q2/2026, doanh thu BSR tăng 60% YoY nhờ giá nhiên liệu tăng mạnh trong 
bối cảnh nguồn cung gián đoạn do xung đột Trung Đông. LNST-CĐTS tăng 807% 
YoY, được hỗ trợ bởi crack spread của bốn sản phẩm lọc dầu chủ lực tăng mạnh do 
nguồn cung thắt chặt.

Xung đột quân sự Mỹ – Iran gây gián đoạn nghiêm trọng dòng chảy dầu qua Eo biển 
Hormuz, thắt chặt nguồn cung dầu thô và thành phẩm, đẩy giá Brent lên quanh 100 
USD/thùng và đưa crack spread tại châu Á đạt đỉnh và duy trì ở vùng cao từ tháng 3 
đến nay. Diễn biến crack spread thời gian tới sẽ phụ thuộc đáng kể vào diễn biến 
chiến sự tại Eo biển Hormuz và tốc độ khôi phục lưu thông qua Eo biển này.

Triển vọng lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn của BSR chịu áp lực khi cán cân cung – cầu 
dầu toàn cầu được dự báo chuyển sang dư cung trong trung và dài hạn. Tuy nhiên, 
nhu cầu tái tích lũy tồn kho dầu toàn cầu có thể phần nào hấp thụ lượng cung dư 
thừa và hạn chế mức độ thu hẹp crack spread.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu BSR chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị Mua với mức giá mục tiêu 32,200 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
16.9% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026. Chúng tôi áp dụng mức EV/EBITDA mục 
tiêu là 15.8 lần, bằng với mức chuẩn trung bình 5 năm của cổ phiếu.

BÁO CÁO NGẮN CƠ HỘI ĐẦU TƯ

15/09/2026

CTCP Nhựa Tiền Phong (NTP) 
Tăng tốc trong chu kỳ thuận lợi

Mua 
Giá mục tiêu VND 73.200 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 1 50.000 – 51.400 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 1 44% 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 2 42.000 – 44.000 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 2 70%

Lưu ý: 
Nhà đầu tư cần kết hợp nhận định xu hướng thị 
trường chung, mức độ hấp dẫn của các điểm 
nhấn đầu tư và khẩu vị rủi ro của mình để quản 
lý tỷ trọng tại từng điểm mua cho phù hợp. 
Do việc xác định mỗi điểm mua được dựa trên 
các khung thời gian khác nhau nên chúng tôi 
không đưa ra mức dừng lỗ cố định. Nhìn chung, 
mức dừng lỗ nên được đặt chặt hơn cho điểm 
mua gần (5-7%) và rộng hơn cho điểm mua xa 
(10-15%).

ĐIỂM NHẤN ĐẦU TƯ 
Kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ tăng trưởng mạnh hơn so với giai đoạn trước. KBSV 
dự báo sản lượng tiêu thụ của NTP tăng 2,3% CAGR giai đoạn 2026 - 2028 nhờ (1) 
Đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư hạ tầng tại miền Bắc (2) gia tăng đầu tư hạ tầng tại các 
địa phương thu hút FDI (3) tăng tốc đơn hàng mảng dự án.

Giá hạt nhựa sẽ gây áp lực biên lợi nhuận nửa cuối 2026 nhưng không làm thay 
đổi triển vọng dài hạn. Mặc dù duy trì chính sách dự phòng nguyên liệu cao và đa 
dạng hóa nhà cung cấp, nhưng NTP vẫn đối mặt với rủi ro thu hẹp lợi nhuận, 
nhưng sẽ không làm giảm đáng kể triển vọng tăng trưởng trung hạn. KBSV cho 
rằng tình trạng dư cung hạt nhựa tại Trung Quốc vẫn sẽ duy trì trong trung hạn và 
xung đột được giải quyết, giảm áp lực biên lợi nhuận toàn ngành.

Catalyst thoái vốn kỳ vọng tái định giá doanh nghiệp. Theo kế hoạch thoái vốn 
Nhà nước, SCIC dự kiến thoái toàn bộ 37,1% cổ phần tại NTP . Chúng tôi đánh giá 
việc hiện thực hóa thương vụ có thể giúp NTP (1) thu hút nhà đầu tư chiến lược 
mới; (2) cộng hưởng mô hình kinh doanh; (3) tái định giá doanh nghiệp.

Hoạt động kinh doanh  Diễn biến doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất

NTP là một trong những doanh nghiệp đầu 
ngành ống nhựa tại Việt Nam, với thị phần 
khoảng 31% toàn quốc và 60% tại miền Bắc. 
Hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi tập trung vào 
sản xuất và phân phối ống nhựa cùng các sản 
phẩm phụ tùng. Nhờ lợi thế về thương hiệu, 
quy mô và mạng lưới phân phối, NTP duy trì 
vị thế dẫn đầu và nền tảng tăng trưởng ổn 
định qua các năm.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KB Securities Vietnam

Tương quan mặt bằng giá  Diễn biến P/E của doanh nghiệp

NTP đang được giao dịch với mức P/E khoảng 
8,3 lần thấp hơn trung bình 5 năm của cổ 
phiếu. Với kỳ vọng doanh nghiệp sẽ bước vào 
pha tăng trưởng mạnh trong 3 năm tới, chúng 
tôi cho rằng P/E fw 2026F ở mức 9,5x lần phù 
hợp với mức dự phóng tăng trưởng 11,6% 
CAGR giai đoạn 2026 – 2028.

Tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 được dự 
báo khả quan so với cùng kỳ

NIM chịu áp lực trước mục tiêu tăng 
trưởng

Chất lư ợng tài s ản suy gi ảm nh ẹ do 
mặt bằng lãi suất ở mức cao

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín d ụng 2026 đ ạt 27%, với động lực chính 
đến từ phân khúc KHDN, đ ặc biệt là t ừ nhóm FDI trong b ối cảnh dòng v ốn FDI vào 
Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực. Xét về lĩnh vực, cho vay kinh 
doanh BĐS kỳ vọng sẽ tăng trưởng vượt trội khi ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp vốn 
cho các dự án hạ tầng và d ự án BĐS ưu tiên , trong khi các m ảng kinh doanh khác 
tăng trưởng ở mức khả quan.

Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM giảm so với năm 2025 do áp l ực huy động khiến chi 
phí vốn duy trì ở mặt bằng cao, trong khi lãi su ất cho vay dự kiến ở mức cạnh tranh 
để ưu tiên mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng.

Chúng tôi dự phóng tỷ lệ nợ xấu của ngân hàng sẽ tăng 11bps YoY trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất đang neo ở mức cao và kỳ vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì chính sách trích 
lập dự phòng thận trọng nhằm củng cố bộ đệm, qua đó hoàn thành mục tiêu duy 
trì tỷ lệ bao phủ nợ xấu ở mức trên 100%.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu MBB với giá mục tiêu 25,950 
VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 26.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 17/09/2026.

Quan điểm kỹ thuật 
Xu hướng & Hành động

Chỉ số VNINDEX

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX cho thấy xu hướng hồi phục trong tuần qua nhưng với diễn biến khá giằng co, 
được thể hiện qua sự hình thành của một mẫu nến "spinning" trên đồ thị tuần. Trên khung 
ngày, bức tranh có phần tích cực hơn khi chỉ số đã cho phản ứng với vùng hỗ trợ quanh 
1790 và hồi phục trở lại, phản ánh sự nhập cuộc khá chủ động của lực cầu. Vùng kháng cự 
gần quanh 1850 bắt đầu khiến áp lực bán có phần gia tăng trong phiên cuối tuần và diễn 
biến rung lắc có thể sẽ còn tiếp diễn. Mặc dù vậy, khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được 
bảo lưu, nhịp giảm điểm vừa qua nhiều khả năng chỉ là bước điều chỉnh tích lũy trước khi 
VNINDEX thử thách lại và có cơ hội vượt vùng đỉnh cũ.

MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.
Exhibit 1: Consumer Spending is Being Supported by Multiple Engines.
A) Jobs growth has stablized. B) Wage growth have helped offset higher prices.

MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: FOMO's Rollercoaster Versus  FEMO's Steady Climb
Date:    5 hours ago
FOMO's Rollercoaster Versus FEMO's Steady Climb

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
FOMO's Rollercoaster Versus
FEMO's Steady Climb
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 19 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Jonny Gios / Unsplash
I. FOMO vs FEMO
Forecasting the stock market is easy! Just multiply the P/E (price-to-
earnings ratio) by E (earnings per share) to get P (the stock price index).
The hard part, of course, is getting the P/E and E right. We call the P/E
component FOMO (fear of missing out) and the E component FEMO
(fabulous earnings momentum). This year, investors have been on a
rollercoaster ride as FOMO has fluctuated mostly to the downside, while

FEMO has continued climbing.

### Page 3

More recently, concerns about AI investment returns and rising US
Treasury yields have tightened credit conditions, raising fears about the
sustainability of the AI investment boom and weighing on P/Es. Since
early October, multiple compression has outpaced continued earnings
gains, dragging many stocks lower.
Semiconductors have been at the center of the recent rout, with the
semiconductor stock price index plunging 15.7% over eight consecutive
sessions. However, FOMO staged a comeback today, with the Nasdaq

rising 1.1% and semiconductors gaining 1.6%, snapping their losing
streak. The FOMO rollercoaster continues, but FEMO remains firmly on
an upward trajectory.
Here is more:
(1) S&P 500. So far this year, forward earnings surged 31.0% ytd through
October 19, more than offsetting a 14.2% contraction in the forward P/E.
Consequently, the index is up 12.3% ytd (chart).
(2) Information Technology. The divergence is even more striking in the
S&P 500 Information Technology sector. Forward earnings has soared

65.5% ytd, while the forward P/E has contracted 25.6%, leaving the
sector's stock price index up 23.0% ytd (chart). Since early October,
however, falling P/Es have outpaced rising earnings, pulling IT stock
prices lower.

### Page 4

(3) Semiconductors. FEMO is incredibly strong in the S&P 500
Semiconductors industry, which now accounts for a record 55.0% of the IT
sector's forward earnings and 40.9% of its market capitalization (chart).
That implies a significant forward P/E discount to the rest of the IT sector
and to the S&P 500. The discount reflects concerns that rapidly rising
borrowing costs for highly leveraged AI infrastructure providers could slow
the AI investment boom and weaken demand for chips. Semiconductor

earnings continue to soar, but that's because capacity constraints are
boosting semiconductor prices, which should fall sooner or later as either
demand slows or capacity expands, or both.
(4) Healthy constraints. We view investors' growing concerns about the
AI boom as good news. Financial, social, political, and physical
constraints should reduce overinvestment risks and keep the boom on a
sustainable path. Recently, financial constraints have been in focus, with

### Page 5

CoreWeave's bond yields surging above 15% last week as the Bond
Vigilantes increasingly scrutinized AI borrowers. The bond market is
playing a welcome role in allocating capital more efficiently. Meanwhile,
the big hyperscalers remain the boom's main drivers, supported by strong
cash flows and ample access to capital. The AI rollercoaster may be
hitting speed bumps, but that will help keep it on a steadier path.
II. Industrial Production
The strength of the US economy continues to underpin strong earnings

growth. Industrial production rose 0.7% m/m in September, its biggest
gain since April, and 2.2% y/y, the strongest since September 2022
(charts). Leading the way higher was utilities output. That reflects rising
electricity demand from data centers and manufacturing, as well as
weather-related demand. Manufacturing output edged up 0.3%.

### Page 6

Since ChatGPT was released in late 2022, technology-related industrial
production has risen sharply to record highs (chart).
Defense and space equipment production also continues to increase at a
solid pace, supported by rising military spending and the rapid growth of
the space industry (chart).

### Page 7

III. Federal Deficit
Fiscal policy also continues to boost economic growth, though at the cost
of large federal deficits and mounting federal debt. The federal budget
deficit totaled $1.99 trillion over the 12 months through September, while
marketable Treasury debt increased by $2.12 trillion (chart).
Federal outlays reached $7.40 trillion, including $3.77 trillion for health
care, Medicare, and Social Security (chart). Notably, net interest payments

reached a record $1.05 trillion, exceeding defense spending of $970
billion.

### Page 8

Tax receipts totaled $5.40 trillion, supported by rising individual income
and payroll taxes as well as tariff revenues (chart). Corporate income tax
receipts have been depressed this year by the 100% depreciation
allowance granted by last year's One Big Beautiful Bill Act (OBBB).
IV. Foreign Capital Flows
Given Corporate America's FEMO, it is not surprising that foreign capital
continues to pour into US financial markets. Over the 12 months through

August, net foreign purchases of US equities totaled a whopping $1.01
trillion, followed by corporate bonds and Treasury notes and bonds (chart).
Apparently, foreign investors have a greater appetite for US equities and
corporate bonds than for US Treasuries.

### Page 9

Net foreign equity inflows surged to a record high, far exceeding previous
peaks (chart). Private investors accounted for $844 billion, while foreign
official institutions contributed another $165 billion, a remarkable
development given their historically limited participation in US equity
markets.
Foreigners purchased more US corporate bonds than US Treasury notes
and bonds over the past 12 months (chart).

### Page 10

clause.

Document 350: Open mouth operations (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Open mouth operations (2026-10-23).pdf

Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

### Page 1

Oil rallied yesterday as markets grow increasingly concerned
about the risk of a renewed escalation in tensions between the
US and Iran

Warren Patterson and Ewa Manthey
Energy – European gas storage draws start
Oil prices surged yesterday, with front-month ICE Brent settling 4%
higher at just above $113/bbl. The rally is being driven less by
current fundamentals than by mounting supply concerns. Markets
are increasingly worried about renewed escalation in the Persian
Gulf, a scenario that could threaten oil ﬂows that have so far
remained remarkably resilient.
Reports suggest President Trump is considering fresh strikes on Iran,
while the US is reinforcing its military presence in the region with the
deployment of a second aircraft carrier. Meanwhile, there are
reports that US troops have been evacuated from some bases in
the Middle East. Clearly, these developments don’t paint an
optimistic picture. They call into question our long-held view that
the US midterm elections would bring de-escalation and

normalisation in ﬂows and oil prices. If anything, re-escalation
seems the most likely path. As a result, our expectation that ICE
Brent would average $80/bbl over 4Q26 is looking unattainable.
Even if tensions don't re-escalate, the mere possibility is likely to
keep a sizeable geopolitical risk premium embedded in prices.
European gas prices also remain extremely sensitive to

developments in the Persian Gulf, with LNG ﬂows increasingly likely
to be largely absent from the market for at least much of the
Northern Hemisphere winter. TTF rallied 3% yesterday, settling
above EUR90/MWh, its highest since December 2022. EU gas
storage also appears to have peaked at just under 75% full, with
storage falling marginally on Tuesday.
Metals — Gold falls as energy risks reignite
inﬂation concerns
Gold prices failed to beneﬁt from yesterday's equity sell-oﬀ and the

signiﬁcant fall in US Treasury yields, with the 10-year yield falling
back below 5%. Instead, gold prices came under pressure. Spot
prices fell 2.8% and are nearing $4,000/oz. The gold market
appears more focused on higher oil prices and what it could mean
for inﬂation — and ultimately central bank policy. With US inﬂation
still above the central bank’s 2% target, higher energy prices could
intensify price pressures and strengthen the case for tighter

monetary policy.
LME copper prices declined for a second consecutive session, falling
more than 1.9% yesterday, as rising exchange inventories and a
selloﬀ in AI-related stocks weighed on broader risk sentiment. LME
data showed copper inventories increased by 27,675 tonnes for a
fourth consecutive session to 272,950 tonnes as of yesterday, the
highest level since 27 July 2026.

Document 387: The Wrap on Asia_ Asia’s goods disinflation over_ (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Wrap on Asia_ Asia’s goods disinflation over_ (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
